pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}})^{2}}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}\nabla_{a}{{\mathcal{E}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=-r^{-2}\nabla_{a}r+2r^{-2}\nabla_{a}r-2r^{-2}\nabla_{a}r+O^{*}(r^{-2-\delta})

\displaystyle=-r^{-2}\nabla_{a}r+O^{*}(r^{-2-\delta}).

(6.31)

We have

n^{a}=\frac{\nabla^{a}r}{|\nabla r|}
, where
|\nabla r|\to 1
as follows from (
2.2
) and (
2.3
). From (
6.31
) we find that

\displaystyle\int_{S_{\infty,\epsilon}}n^{a}{{\Psi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}\,d\mu

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{\infty,\epsilon}}(-\epsilon^{2}+O(\epsilon^{2+\delta}))\,d\mu

\displaystyle=-(1+O(\epsilon^{\delta}))\epsilon^{2}\mathrm{Vol}(S_{\infty,\epsilon},g)

\displaystyle=-(1+O(\epsilon^{\delta}))\epsilon^{2}\mathrm{Vol}(L,\sigma_{\epsilon^{-1}})

(6.32)

The manifold
L
is a circle fibration over the orbifold
O
. From Definition
2.1
we have that the transverse metric
\gamma
gives a metric on
O
with constant curvature
+1
. With respect to this fibration, the metric
\sigma_{r}=r^{2}\gamma+\eta^{2}
, and its volume form is
d\mu^{\sigma_{r}}=r^{2}d\mu^{\gamma}\wedge\eta
. By Fubini’s theorem and the orbifold Gauss–Bonnet theorem
[
52
, S 2]
, the volume of
L
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vol}(L,\sigma_{\epsilon^{-1}})

\displaystyle=\int_{O}\left(\epsilon^{-2}\int_{\text{fiber}}\eta\right)d\mu^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon^{-2}\ell_{\infty}\int_{O}1\,d\mu^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-2}\ell_{\infty}\int_{O}\mathcal{K}^{O}\,d\mu^{\gamma}

\displaystyle=2\pi\epsilon^{-2}\ell_{\infty}\chi[O].

(6.33)

We conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{S_{\infty,\epsilon}}n^{a}{{\Psi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}\,d\mu\leq-2\pi\ell_{\infty}\chi[O].

(6.34)

∎

6.3.
The singular term

Lemma 6.13

(The singular term)
.

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{\partial U_{{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{489616}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{563125}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}},\epsilon}}n^{a}{{\Psi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}\,d\mu=0.

(6.35)

Proof.

Proposition
6.3
tells us that
\lambda
is bounded away from zero near
{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}
. From (
5.25
) and (
5.8c
) we therefore see that
|n^{a}{{\Psi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}|\leq C
independently of
\epsilon>0
near
{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\partial U_{{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{489616}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{563125}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}},\epsilon}}n^{a}{{\Psi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}\,d\mu\right|\leq C\operatorname{Vol}\left(\partial U_{{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{489616}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{563125}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}},\epsilon}\right).

(6.36)

By (
6.6
) we have for sufficiently small
\delta>0

\displaystyle\operatorname{Vol}\left(\partial U_{{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.87502pt}{\resizebox{485301}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{489616}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{563125}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}},\epsilon}\right)\leq D\sum_{i}r_{i}^{3}\leq D\sum_{i}r_{i}^{2+\delta}<D\epsilon,

(6.37)

and the claim follows.
∎

Remark 6.14
.

The singular set
{{\mathcal{Z}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}
was also an issue in the black hole uniqueness proofs carried out on the orbit space in the hypersurface-orthogonal case, as sketched in Section
1
. While in his proof Israel
[
33
]
assumed the absence of such a set, the latter was admitted and subject of a technically subtle discussion in Müller zum Hagen et. al.
[
44
]
.
On the other hand, we are not aware of suitable generalizations of Robinson’s identities
[
51
]
, which avoid the singular set, to the non-hypersurface orthogonal case.

6.4.
Proof of Proposition
6.7

Proposition
6.7
follows from Lemmas
5.10
,
6.9
,
6.10
,
6.12
,
6.13
.

7.
Proof of the main theorem

In this section, we will analyze the terms in the divergence identity derived in Proposition
6.7
and prove our main theorem. The first step is the following corollary to Proposition
6.7
.

Definition 7.1
.

For a nut
P
with fixed point data
\{\epsilon,a,b\}
with
a\leq b
, let

\displaystyle{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P)=\pm\epsilon\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}.

(7.1)

Corollary 7.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
be an ALF-
A_{k}

S^{1}
-instanton which is not half-flat. Let
w\geq 1
be the maximal weight of all nuts in
\mathcal{M}
. If
\mathcal{M}
has no nuts, we set
w=1
. It holds that

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{w}+\chi[\mathcal{M}]-n_{\text{\rm nuts}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\geq 0

(7.2)

with equality if and only if
{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}
is algebraically special.

Proof.

Since
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is ALF-
A_{k}
we have
\chi[O]=2
. Let
G
be as in Lemma
3.1
. Divide (
6.11
) by
4\pi^{2}G
. The corollary then follows from (
3.7
) and Lemma
6.11
.
∎

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
P
be a nut. If
\epsilon(P)=1
, then
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P)\leq 2
and
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P)\leq 0
. If
\epsilon(P)=-1
, then
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P)\leq 2
and
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P)\leq 0
. For any of the inequalities, equality holds if and only if the weights of
P
are
\{1,1\}
.

Proof.

Let
P
have weights
a,b
, where
a\leq b
. If
\epsilon(P)=1
, then

\displaystyle{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P)=\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b},

(7.3)

and since
a
and
b
are positive integers, we have
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P)\leq 2
with equality if and only if
a=b=1
. Since
a\leq b
we see that

\displaystyle{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P)=-\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}\leq 0

(7.4)

where equality occurs if and only if
a=b
, which happens only when
a=b=1
since
a
and
b
are coprime. The reasoning in the case
\epsilon(P)=-1
is similar.
∎

We are now ready to apply inequality (
7.2
) to known spaces with ALF-
A_{k}

S^{1}
-instanton metrics. We first consider the case with Kerr topology.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
be an AF
S^{1}
-instanton on
S^{4}\setminus S^{1}
. Then equality holds in (
7.2
), for both signs.

Proof.

In the present case we have
e=0
,
\chi[\mathcal{M}]=2
and
\mathop{\rm sign}[\mathcal{M}]=0
. Equation (
3.15
) with
e=0
tells us that the number of positively oriented nuts is equal to the number of negatively oriented nuts. We denote this number by
k
. Corollary
7.2
yields

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{w}+2-2k+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\geq 0.

(7.5)

In case there are no nuts,
w=1
and
k=0
, and we have equality in (
7.5
) and hence in
(
7.2
).

We now assume
k>0
.
In the case
w=1
, all weights are
1
, so we get
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})=\pm\epsilon(P_{i})+1
for all
i
, and thus the left hand side of (
7.5
) is equal to

\displaystyle-2+2-2k+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}(\pm\epsilon(P_{i})+1)=\pm\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}\epsilon(P_{i})=0.

(7.6)

This means that equality holds in (
7.5
) and (
7.2
), for both signs.

Now consider the case
w>1
, and assume without loss of generality that
P_{1}
has nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),a,w\}
for some positive integer
a
. By Lemma
3.5
, there exists another nut, with nut data either
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),a,w\}
or
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
, where
a+b=w
; without loss of generality, assume that this nut is
P_{2}
.
In the case where
P_{2}
has nut data
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),a,w\}
, we have

\displaystyle{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{1})+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{2})=\pm\frac{\epsilon(P_{1})}{a}+\frac{1}{w}\mp\frac{\epsilon(P_{1})}{a}+\frac{1}{w}=\frac{2}{w},

(7.7)

and then (
7.5
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 2-2k+\sum_{i=3}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\geq 0.

(7.8)

On the other hand, since the set
\{P_{3},\dots,P_{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}\}
contains
k-1
nuts of each orientation, Lemma
7.3
implies that

\displaystyle 2-2k+\sum_{i=3}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq 0

(7.9)

which means that equality must hold in view of Lemma
7.3
. Consider now the case where
P_{2}
has nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
, with
a+b=w
. Then

\displaystyle{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{1})+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{2})=\frac{2}{w}\pm\epsilon(P_{1})\left(\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}\right),

(7.10)

so that (
7.5
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\pm\epsilon(P_{1})\left(\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}\right)+2-2k+\sum_{i=3}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\geq 0.

(7.11)

From (
3.15
) we find that the set
\{P_{3},\dots,P_{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}\}
must contain
k-2
(
k\geq 2
) nuts with the same orientation as
P_{1}
, and
k
nuts with the opposite orientation. Assuming that
\epsilon(P_{3})=\epsilon(P_{4})=-\epsilon(P_{1})
, Lemma
7.3
tells us that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq 2k-4.

(7.12)

In case
\epsilon(P_{1})=1
, thus, the
+
version of (
7.11
) implies that

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{3})+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{4})-2\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}-2\leq 0,

(7.13)

where the middle inequality uses again Lemma
7.3
. Thus,
a=b=1
and
{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{3})={{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{4})=0
, which, once again by Lemma
7.3
, means that
P_{3}
and
P_{4}
have weights
\{1,1\}
. In the case
\epsilon(P_{1})=-1
, similarly, the
-
version of (
7.11
) implies that

\displaystyle 0\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{3})+{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{4})-2\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{1}{b}-2\leq 0.

(7.14)

In either case, thus,
a=b=1
, and
P_{3}
and
P_{4}
have weights
\{1,1\}
. Hence the left hand side of (
7.5
) is equal to

\displaystyle\pm 2\epsilon(P_{1})+2(\mp\epsilon(P_{1})+1)+2-2k+\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}(P_{i})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2+2-2k+(2k-4)=0,

(7.15)

which finishes the proof.
∎

We next consider the case of Taub-bolt topology, which turns out to be rather similar to the Kerr case.

Lemma 7.5
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
be an ALF-
A_{0}

S^{1}
-instanton on
\mathbb{C}P^{2}\setminus\{\text{\rm pt.}\}
. Then equality holds in (
7.2
), for both signs.

Proof.

From the assumption on the topology, we have
\chi[\mathcal{M}]=2
and
\mathop{\rm sign}[\mathcal{M}]=1
, and since
\mathcal{M}
is ALF-
A_{0}
, the circle fibration at infinity has Euler number
e=1
. By (
3.15
) we see that, as in the proof of the previous lemma, there is an equal number of positively and negatively oriented nuts. The rest of that proof goes through without any modification.
∎

Finally, we consider the case of an
S^{1}
-instanton with the same topology as the Chen–Teo instanton.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
be an AF
S^{1}
-instanton on
\mathbb{C}P^{2}\setminus S^{1}
. Then equality holds in (
7.2
), for
-
.

Proof.

Let
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}
be the left hand side of (
7.2
), so
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}\geq 0
by Corollary
7.2
. We wish to show that
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=0
. By the assumption on the topology, we have
\chi[\mathcal{M}]=3
and
\mathop{\rm sign}[\mathcal{M}]=1
. From (
3.15
) we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}\epsilon(P_{i})=1,

(7.16)

and thus the numbers of positively oriented and negatively oriented nuts are
k+1
and
k
, respectively, for some integer
k\geq 0
. If all nuts have weights
\{1,1\}
, then
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=0
and we are done, so assume that
w>1
, where
w
is the largest weight, and assume without loss of generality that
P_{1}
has this weight; then
P_{1}
has nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
for some
b
. If there exists another nut with nut data
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
, then the same argument as in the proof of (
7.9
) works to show that
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}\leq 0
, since there are
k-1
remaining negatively oriented nuts in this case.

Thus, assume that there exists no nut with nut data
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
. By Lemma
3.4
there exists another nut with nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),a,w\}
, where
a+b=w
. Assume without loss of generality that this nut is
P_{2}
, and that
b\geq a
. If
a=b=1
and
\epsilon(P_{1})=1
, then using Lemma
7.3
we see that

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=-2+2-2k+\sum_{i=3}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-2+2-2k+2k=0.

(7.17)

If
a=b=1
and
\epsilon(P_{1})=-1
, we similarly find

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=2+2-2k+\sum_{i=3}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq 2+2-2k+2(k-2)=0.

(7.18)

Henceforth, we can thus assume that
b>a
. By applying Lemma
3.4
to the nut
P_{1}
, there is a nut with data either
\{\epsilon(P_{1}),b,c\}
, where
a+b\equiv-c\mod{b}
, or data
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),b,c\}
, where
a+b\equiv c\mod{b}
. Since
b
is equal to neither
a
nor
a+b
, this nut cannot be
P_{2}
, so we can assume without loss of generality that it is
P_{3}
. If
\epsilon(P_{3})=\epsilon(P_{1})
, then since
a+c\equiv a+b+c\equiv 0\mod{b}
and since
0<a+c<b+(a+b)<3b
, we have
c\in\{b-a,2b-a\}
. On the other hand, if
\epsilon(P_{3})=-\epsilon(P_{1})
, then since
a-c\equiv a+b-c\equiv 0\mod{b}
and since
a+b
is by assumption the highest weight in the configuration, we have
c\in\{a+b,a\}
. However, the case
c=a+b
in which
P_{3}
would have nut data
\{-\epsilon(P_{1}),b,w\}
has also been excluded by assumption above. This implies
c=a
.

Together with the possibilities of the choice of
\epsilon(P_{1})
, we are left with six cases each of which will be considered separately. In the list below, the ordering of the nuts is
P_{1}
,
P_{2}
and
P_{3}
, and the weights of each nut are displayed in increasing order.

Case:
\{+,b,a+b\},\{+,a,a+b\}\{+,b-a,b\}

By Lemma
7.3
we have

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{b-a}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{b-a}-2,

(7.19)

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{a}-\frac{1}{b-a}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-\frac{1}{a}-\frac{1}{b-a}+2.

(7.20)

The right hand side of (
7.19
) is negative for
b>3
which is thus impossible. The remaining cases
(a,b)=(1,2)
,
(a,b)=(1,3)
and
(a,b)=(2,3)
will be handled separately. When
(a,b)=(1,2)
, (
7.20
) yields
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=0
and we are done.

When
(a,b)=(1,3)
, we have the nut configuration

\displaystyle\{+,3,4\},\quad\{+,1,4\},\quad\{+,2,3\},

(7.21)

and since the weight
2
occurs only once in this configuration, by Lemma
3.3
there must exist another nut, say
P_{4}
, with nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{4}),2,c\}
for some
c
. If
\epsilon(P_{4})=1
, then

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}=\frac{8}{3}+\frac{1}{c}+2-2k+\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{c}<0

(7.22)

which is a contradiction, while if
\epsilon(P_{4})=-1
, then

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=-1+\frac{1}{c}+2-2k+\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-1+\frac{1}{c}\leq 0

(7.23)

and we conclude that
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=0
.

Finally, when
(a,b)=(2,3)
, we have the nut configuration

\displaystyle\{+,3,5\},\quad\{+,2,5\},\quad\{+,1,3\},

(7.24)

and as in the previous case, there is a nut
P_{4}
with data
\{\epsilon(P_{4}),2,c\}
. Reasoning as in the previous case, we find that
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}<0
or
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}<0
holds, depending on whether
\epsilon(P_{4})=1
or
\epsilon(P_{4})=-1
.

Case:
\{+,b,a+b\},\{+,a,a+b\},\{+,b,2b-a\}

In this case,

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}=\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{2b-a}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{2b-a}-2,

(7.25)

and the right hand side is negative for
b>2
, which rules out this case.

For the case
(a,b)=(1,2)
, we have the nut configuration

\displaystyle\{+,2,3\},\quad\{+,1,3\},\quad\{+,2,3\},

(7.26)

and since the weight
3
occurs an odd number of times, Lemma
3.3
tells us that there exists another nut, say
P_{4}
, with nut data
\{\epsilon(P_{4}),3,c\}
, for some
c
. If
\epsilon(P_{4})=1
, then

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}=\frac{8}{3}+\frac{1}{c}+2-2k+\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.76923pt}{\resizebox{481531}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{516533}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{583338}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{c}<0,

(7.27)

while if
\epsilon(P_{4})=-1
, then

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=-\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{c}+2-2k+\sum_{i=5}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq-\frac{4}{3}+\frac{1}{c}<0,

(7.28)

which leads to a contradiction.

Case:
\{+,b,a+b\},\{+,a,a+b\},\{-,a,b\}

In this case,

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})+2-2k\leq 0,

(7.29)

so
{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=0
.

Case:
\{-,b,a+b\},\{-,a,a+b\},\{-,b-a,b\}

In this case,

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{b-a}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{b-a}-4<0,

(7.30)

which is impossible.

Case:
\{-,b,a+b\},\{-,a,a+b\},\{-,b,2b-a\}

In this case,

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{2b-a}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq\frac{1}{a}+\frac{2}{b}+\frac{1}{2b-a}-4<0,

(7.31)

which is a contradiction.

Case:
\{-,b,a+b\},\{-,a,a+b\},\{+,a,b\}

In this case,

\displaystyle{{\Phi}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}=\frac{2}{b}+2-2k+\sum_{i=4}^{n_{\text{\rm nuts}}}{{Z}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{392747}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{429219}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle-$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{519722}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}}}}}}(P_{i})\leq\frac{2}{b}-2<0,

(7.32)

which again is a contradiction.
∎

7.1.
Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

First assume that
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is an ALF
S^{1}
-instanton on
S^{4}\setminus S^{1}
. Then, by Lemma
7.4
, we have that equality holds in (
7.2
) for both signs. By Corollary
7.2
, it follows that
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is Petrov type D and hence toric
[
3
]
. The classification of
[
8
]
now applies to show that
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is in the Kerr family. Similarly, if
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is an ALF
S^{1}
-instanton on
\CP^{2}\setminus\{\text{\rm pt.}\}
, we have by Lemma
7.5
that it is Petrov type D and by a similar argument toric and hence by
[
8
]
,
(\mathcal{M},g_{ab})
is in the Taub-bolt family. Finally, Lemma
7.6
gives point
3
of Theorem
1.1
, in particular an AF
S^{1}
-instanton on
\CP^{2}\setminus S^{1}
is one-sided type D and therefore Hermitian.

8.
Concluding remarks

In this paper we have applied the divergence identity derived in Section
5
to the case of ALF
S^{1}
-instantons, restricting to those topologies where there are known Hermitian examples. However, it is worth emphasizing that the method can be applied more broadly. The
G
-signature can, together with the approach of Jang, be used to classify
S^{1}
-actions on gravitational instantons. This allows one to apply the divergence identity to general
S^{1}
-instantons. These applications will be considered in forthcoming work.

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
4.3

First we recall the form of partly contracted volume forms,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{abcd}\epsilon^{d}{}_{efh}={}

\displaystyle g_{ah}g_{bf}g_{ce}-g_{af}g_{bh}g_{ce}-g_{ah}g_{be}g_{cf}

\displaystyle+g_{ae}g_{bh}g_{cf}+g_{af}g_{be}g_{ch}-g_{ae}g_{bf}g_{ch},

(A.1)

\displaystyle\epsilon_{abcd}\epsilon^{cd}{}_{fh}={}

\displaystyle-2g_{ah}g_{bf}+2g_{af}g_{bh}.

(A.2)

By (

), the SD/ASD field
\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{ab}{}^{cd}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{cd}=\pm{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}
satisfies

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{b}{}_{c}={}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\epsilon_{ab}{}^{ef}\epsilon^{b}{}_{c}{}^{hi}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ef}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{hi}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{4}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}g_{ac}.

(A.3)

By (

),

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}{{\sigma}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{b}={}

\displaystyle 2{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{b}{}_{c}\xi^{c}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}\xi_{a},

(A.4)

which is (
4.6a
) and similarly, (
4.6b
) follows via

\displaystyle{{\sigma}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{a}{{\sigma}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{a}={}

\displaystyle 4{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{a}{}_{c}\xi^{b}\xi^{c}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}\lambda.

(A.5)

By Ricci flatness, we have
\nabla_{b}F_{ac}=\nabla_{b}\nabla_{a}\xi_{c}=-W_{acbd}\xi^{d}
, see
[
50
, Proposition 8.1.3]
, and hence (
4.6c
) follows. This also leads to (

) via

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}={}

\displaystyle 2{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}\nabla_{c}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}\xi^{d}.

(A.6)

To prove (

), it is convenient to start with its dual,

\displaystyle\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}\nabla_{a}{{\sigma}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{b}={}

\displaystyle 2\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}\nabla_{a}\left({{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{bc}\xi^{c}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{bcad}\xi^{c}\xi^{d}}_{=0}+2\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{bc}F_{a}{}^{c}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{2\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}F_{bc}F_{a}{}^{c}}_{=0}\pm\epsilon_{fh}{}^{ab}\epsilon_{bc}{}^{ij}F_{ij}F_{a}{}^{c}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0.

(A.7)

The last step used (
A.1
) and dualizing the equation shows (

). The divergence, (

), follows from

\displaystyle\nabla_{a}{{\sigma}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{a}={}

\displaystyle 2\nabla_{a}\left({{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}\xi_{b}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-2{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}{}_{ad}\xi_{b}\xi^{d}}_{=0}+2{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}F_{ab}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}\left({{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{ab}+{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\mp$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\mp$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\mp$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mp$}}}}}}}_{ab}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}.

(A.8)

Finally, to prove (
4.6g
), apply a derivative to (

),

\displaystyle\nabla^{c}\nabla_{c}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{2}={}

\displaystyle-2\nabla^{c}\left({{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}\xi^{d}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-2\big(\underbrace{(\nabla^{c}{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd})}_{=0}\xi^{d}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}+{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}F^{cd}-\underbrace{{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\ \displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{ab}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}^{cd}-\underbrace{{{\mathcal{W}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\textstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{451967}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.40833pt}{$\scriptstyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.49998pt}{\resizebox{510420}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\pm$}}}}}}}_{abcd}{{\mathcal{F}}^{\hskip-0.5pt\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{229376}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83334pt}{$\displaystyle\pm$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.67308pt}{\resizebox{427220}{
